[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{x}{b}}}}=\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{y}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{y}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{y}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{y}{b}}}}=\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{x}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{x}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{x}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{x}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}=\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{y}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{y}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{y}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}{\Gamma^{{{y}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}}}}=0
, and we can
identify
\mathrm{D}_{\alpha}=\nabla_{\alpha}
and
\mathring{\mathrm{D}}_{i}=\nabla_{i}=\partial_{i}
, and thus, in the following we will use the covariant
derivative symbol instead of the roman D.

Tensor sector

The harmonic equation for the tensor harmonic:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{T}_{xx}}{\partial x^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{T}_{xx}}{\partial y^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\mathbb{T}_{xx}=0,

(3.66a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{T}_{xy}}{\partial x^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{T}_{xy}}{\partial y^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\mathbb{T}_{xy}=0,

(3.66b)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{T}_{yy}}{\partial x^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{T}_{yy}}{\partial y^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\mathbb{T}_{yy}=0,

(3.66c)

with
k_{\perp}>0
. Now, as all of these have the same form, we know the components are going to share the eigenfunction, namely
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}
, where
\mathbf{k}_{\perp}=(k_{x},k_{y})
is such that
k_{\perp}^{2}=k_{x}^{2}+k_{y}^{2}
. We can now use the tracelessness and the
symmetry conditions to see that we will have

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{xx}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\mathbb{T}_{yy}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=A_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{xy}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=\mathbb{T}_{yx}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=B_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

(3.67)

where
A_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}
and
B_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}
are constants to be determined. On the other hand, the transversality of these tensors implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{T}_{xx}}{\partial x}+\frac{\mathbb{T}_{yx}}{\partial y}=\mathrm{i}\left(k_{x}A_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+k_{y}B_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}=0,

(3.68a)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{T}_{xy}}{\partial x}+\frac{\mathbb{T}_{yy}}{\partial y}=\mathrm{i}\left(k_{x}B_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-k_{y}A_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}=0,

(3.68b)

which are only satisfied
A_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=B_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=0
, this is,
there is no tensor harmonic associated to the 2-dimensional plane
. This
does not imply immediately that
\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}(\lambda,\xi)=0
, and we should analyze the corresponding equation for this amplitude.
Eq. (
3.48
) in Rindler spacetime reads

\mathrm{e}^{-2a\xi}\left[-\frac{\partial^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}}{\partial\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}}{\partial\xi^{2}}\right]-k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}=0,

(3.69)

whose solutions can be expanded in terms of the normalizable eigenfunctions
4
4

4

Strictly speaking, the modified Bessel function of the first kind
\mathrm{I}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)
should also be present but these are omitted because they are not normalizable.

\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}=T_{\omega k_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a),

(3.70)

where
\omega\geq 0
will be identified as the Rindler energy carried by the mode, and
T_{\omega k_{\perp}}
is a constant to be determined.

Vector sector

For the vector harmonics on the plane we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{V}_{x}}{\partial x^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{V}_{x}}{\partial y^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{x}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{V}_{y}}{\partial x^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{V}_{y}}{\partial y^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{y}

\displaystyle=0.

(3.71)

The transversality condition
\nabla^{i}\mathbb{V}_{i}=0
is identically satisfied if we use the solutions
5
5

5

In this entire section we do not pay any mind
to the normalization of the harmonics, as we will require that the modes be normalized in the end.

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{x}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=k_{y}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{y}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=-k_{x}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}.

(3.72)

In covariant notation, we can use the Levi-Civita pseudo-tensor of the
xy
-plane whose only nonzero components are
\epsilon_{xy}=-\epsilon_{yx}=1
:

\mathbb{V}_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{ij}\nabla^{j}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}=\epsilon_{ij}k_{\perp}^{j}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}.

(3.73)

Using the definition of Eq. (
3.50
), we find the traceless tensor associated to the vector harmonic

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{xx}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\frac{k_{x}k_{y}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{xy}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{k_{x}^{2}-k_{y}^{2}}{2k_{\perp}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{yy}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\frac{k_{x}k_{y}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}.

(3.74)

On the other hand, the field equation for the vector sector master variable is

\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}-k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}=\mathrm{e}^{-2a\xi}\left[-\frac{\partial^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}}{\partial\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}}{\partial\xi^{2}}\right]-k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}=0,

(3.75)

which is the same form as Eq. (
3.69
), and thus the solutions can be expanded in terms of functions of the same
form as in Eq. (
3.70
), but with a different constant in front:

\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}=V_{\omega k_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a).

(3.76)

which will be set by normalization. Note we are using the same frequency
\omega
, as there are no physical arguments to justify a mode carrying more than one
energy level in the Rindler observer’s perspective. From Eq. (
3.53
) we see that in Rindler spacetime

f_{\alpha}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}-\frac{1}{k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}^{\omega k_{\perp}},

(3.77)

is the gauge dependent variable associated to the vector sector. The tensor perturbation is then written as

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=0,

(3.78a)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha i}=f^{\omega k_{\perp}}_{\alpha}(\lambda,\xi)\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}),

(3.78b)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=2\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}(\lambda,\xi)\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}).

(3.78c)

Here we see the tensor master variable influences the vector sector.

Using Eq. (
3.49
b), the definition of
Eq. (
3.77
) and the dynamical
equation (
3.69
) we can see
the vector sector modes are both traceless and transverse by construction
.

Scalar sector

The equation governing the behavior of the scalar harmonic is

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{S}}{\partial x^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{S}}{\partial y^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\mathbb{S}=0,

(3.79)

which has solutions of the type

\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

(3.80)

from where we define the corresponding vector with components

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{x}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\frac{k_{x}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{y}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\frac{k_{y}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

(3.81)

and the tensor from the definition of Eq. (
3.58
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{xx}

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{k_{x}^{2}-k_{y}^{2}}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{xy}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{x}k_{y}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{yy}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{k_{x}^{2}-k_{y}^{2}}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}.

(3.82)

is the 2-tensor associated to the scalar harmonic.

We can quickly find the auxiliary vector (
3.62
) using the results of the vector sector, this is, the corresponding master variable

\displaystyle X_{\alpha}^{\omega k_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{\perp}}\left(f_{\alpha}^{\omega k_{\perp}}+\frac{1}{k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}\right)=\frac{1}{k_{\perp}}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}},

(3.83)

while the scalar master variable
\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}
satisfies the field equation

\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}=\mathrm{e}^{-2a\xi}\left[-\frac{\partial^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}}{\partial\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}}{\partial\xi^{2}}\right]-k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}=0,

(3.84)

and it is useful to find the gauge invariants

\displaystyle F=\frac{k^{2}_{\perp}}{4}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}},

(3.85a)

\displaystyle F_{\alpha\beta}=\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-\frac{k^{2}_{\perp}}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}.

(3.85b)

Then, from the definitions (
3.61
) we find

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{l}}=\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{4}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}},

(3.86)

\displaystyle f_{\alpha\beta}=\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-\frac{1}{k_{\perp}}\left\{\epsilon_{\beta\gamma}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\gamma}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}+\epsilon_{\alpha\gamma}\nabla_{\beta}\nabla^{\gamma}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}\right\}.

(3.87)

Then, the scalar sector modes can simply be written as

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=f^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}_{\alpha\beta}(\lambda,\xi)\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}),

(3.88a)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha i}=f^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}_{\alpha}(\lambda,\xi)\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}),

(3.88b)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=2\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}(\lambda,\xi)\mathbb{S}_{ij}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}).

(3.88c)

Note that by construction
g^{\alpha\beta}f_{\alpha\beta}=0
, and as the harmonic tensor defined in
Eq. (
3.82
) is also traceless, the tracelessness condition for the entire perturbation is satisfied if
\Omega_{\mathrm{l}}=0
. The divergence of the modes is also straightforward to compute, we have

\nabla^{a}h_{a\beta}^{(\mathrm{s})}=\nabla_{\beta}\!\left[\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}\right]\mathbb{S},

(3.89)

and

\nabla^{a}h_{aj}^{(\mathrm{s})}=2\Omega_{\mathrm{l}}\mathbb{S}_{j},

(3.90)

so if we impose the TT gauge we obtain the conditions

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}-\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}

\displaystyle=\text{constant},

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{l}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.91)

which are consistent between them, and following Eq. (
3.86
) yield the relation between the scalar and tensor master variables:

\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}=\frac{2}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}=\frac{2T_{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)=S_{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a).

(3.92)

We can see from Eqs. (
3.78
) and (
3.88
) that the tensor master variable appears in both the
sectors; the same can be said about the vector master variable. This means the sectors are coupled between each other. We can break this coupling by modifying
the modes through gauge transformations.

3.3.2
Decoupling and normalization of the Rindler modes

Using the definition of the inner product (
3.10
) and the generalized momentum in the traceless and transverse gauge
(
3.12
), we find that the generalized Klein-Gordon inner product in Rindler spacetime is given by

\langle h^{(1)},h^{(2)}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\kappa^{2}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}yW_{\lambda}[h^{(1)},h^{(2)}],

(3.93)

where we used
\mathrm{d}^{3}\Sigma^{a}=\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}(+\mathrm{e}^{-a\xi},0,0,0)\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y
as the future oriented 3-volume element and defined the
current

W_{b}[h^{(1)},h^{(2)}]\coloneqq\overline{h^{cd}_{(1)}}\nabla_{b}h^{(2)}_{cd}-h^{cd}_{(2)}\nabla_{b}\overline{h^{(1)}_{cd}}.

(3.94)

As the modes we have found are in the TT gauge, normalization seems straightforward using Eq. (
3.93
). The catch is that these,
as presented, are quite complicated since the scalar and vector sectors are intertwined, due to the fact that the vector and tensor master variables appear in
both sets of modes. Fortunately, there are gauge transformations that allow us to decouple these, reducing the complexity of the modes, while keeping the modes
traceless and transverse.

In order to proceed with this, let us first note that the decomposition of the modes in terms of the scalar, vector and tensor sectors is
unique
[
80
]
, and thus, given a gauge transformation we will be able to identify the tensor, vector and scalar sectors
of the resulting perturbation. We also have that the rank-1 tensors that characterize gauge transformations can be decomposed using scalar and vector harmonics,
this is, for an arbitrary vector
\Lambda_{a}

\Lambda_{a}=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\left(\Lambda_{a}^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}+\Lambda_{a}^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}\right),

(3.95)

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\alpha}^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=\phi^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{\alpha}(\lambda,\xi)\ \mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=\varphi^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(\lambda,\xi)\ \mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}),

(3.96)

and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\alpha}^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=\psi^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(\lambda,\xi)\,\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp}).

(3.97)

Here, the auxiliary fields
\phi^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{\alpha}
,
\varphi^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}
and
\psi^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}
are defined in the orbit.

As derivatives do not mix the components that arise from the geometry of the maximally symmetric space (e.g., we will not find vector harmonics, or the tensor
that arises from them, by deriving the scalar harmonic), an important result of this decomposition is that
\Lambda_{a}^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}
will only
act on the scalar sector, and
\Lambda_{a}^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}
will only act on the vector sector, meaning we can consider gauge transformations
independently in each sector, using the definitions of Eqs. (
3.96
) and (
3.97
). Using these, we
find that the vector modes of Eq. (
3.78
) are transformed as

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(\mathrm{v})}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(\mathrm{v})}_{\alpha i}

\displaystyle=(f_{\alpha}-\nabla_{\alpha}\psi)\mathbb{V}_{i},

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(\mathrm{v})}_{ij}

\displaystyle=2(\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}+k_{\perp}\psi)\mathbb{V}_{ij},

(3.98)

while the scalar sector is transformed as

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(\mathrm{s})}_{\alpha\beta}=(f_{\alpha\beta}-2\nabla_{(\alpha}\phi_{\beta)})\mathbb{S},

(3.99a)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(\mathrm{s})}_{\alpha i}=(f_{\alpha}-\nabla_{\alpha}\varphi+k_{\perp}\phi_{\alpha})\mathbb{S}_{i},

(3.99b)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(\mathrm{s})}_{ij}=-k_{\perp}\varphi g_{ij}\mathbb{S}+2(\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}+k_{\perp}\varphi)\mathbb{S}_{ij}.

(3.99c)

If we choose our gauge functions to be
\psi=-\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}/k_{\perp}
,
\varphi=0
, and
\phi_{\alpha}=-\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}/k_{\perp}
, the modes remain in the TT gauge and are given by

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{V}_{j}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.100)

for the vector sector
6
6

6

These are proportional to the odd modes previously reported by

\Citeauthor
sugiyamaGravitationalWavesKasner2021
[
41
]
.
, and

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}g_{\alpha\beta}\right)\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.101a)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}=-\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{j},

(3.101b)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}_{ij}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.101c)

where we drop the tilde as the distinction between both sets of modes will not be needed anymore: we will drop the old form and proceed to work with these only.
We show these new modes are both traceless and transverse in section
B.1
of the appendix.

Having the modes in the TT gauge, it is straightforward to compute Eq. (
3.93
) to find the constants
V_{\omega k_{\perp}}
and
S_{\omega k_{\perp}}
defined in Eqs. (
3.76
)
and (
3.92
) respectively. However, this calculation is very laborious (specially the normalization of the scalar
sector), and it does not add value to the current discussion to reproduce it integrally here, therefore to avoid losing the attention of the reader we have
relegated it to the appendix, specifically, section
B.2
. However it is very important to at least give the results here:

\displaystyle V_{\omega k_{\perp}}=\frac{\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}},

(3.102)

\displaystyle S_{\omega k_{\perp}}=\frac{\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{a\pi^{4}}}.

(3.103)

With these amplitudes, the Rindler modes satisfy the normalization relation

\langle h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\,\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}),

(3.104)

where we used
\mathrm{p}
to label the different sectors:
\mathrm{p},\mathrm{p}^{\prime}\in\{\mathrm{s},\mathrm{v}\}
. An analogous calculation shows

\displaystyle\langle\overline{h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}},\overline{h^{(\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}}\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=-\,\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\,\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\langle h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},\overline{h^{(\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}}\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.105)

therefore we can conclude that the tensor Rindler modes here defined (and their complex conjugates) form a complete and orthonormal basis for rank
\binom{0}{2}

tensors in Rindler spacetime.

3.3.3
Minkowski modes

We will reuse the discussion about the harmonics of section (
3.3.1
), as Minkowski spacetime in Cartesian coordinates is the same manifold:
\mathbb{R}^{4}=\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where the orbit in this case is simply 2-dimensional Minkowski, meaning the metric is defined from

\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2}=-\mathrm{d}t^{2}+\mathrm{d}z^{2},

(3.106)

and the second subspace corresponds with the plane. Moreover, the gauge transformed perturbations of Eqs. (
3.100
)
and (
3.101
) have been obtained from the properties of the harmonics and in covariant fashion, therefore, we can use an analogous
procedure and still remain in the TT gauge to find that the perturbations can be written as

\displaystyle H^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k})}_{\alpha\beta}=0,

(3.107a)

\displaystyle H^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k})}_{\alpha j}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\Psi_{\mathrm{v}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}\mathbb{V}_{j}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.107b)

\displaystyle H^{(\mathrm{v},\mathbf{k})}_{ij}=0,

(3.107c)

and

\displaystyle H^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k})}_{\alpha\beta}=\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}g_{\alpha\beta}\right)\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.108a)

\displaystyle H^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k})}_{\alpha j}=\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{j},

(3.108b)

H^{(\mathrm{s},\mathbf{k})}_{ij}=k_{\perp}^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}\mathbb{S}_{ij}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.108c)

where the inertial master variables are given by

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathrm{v}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{4k_{\perp}^{2}\sqrt{\pi^{3}k_{0}}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(-k_{0}t+k_{z}z)},

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2k_{\perp}^{2}\sqrt{\pi^{3}k_{0}}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(-k_{0}t+k_{z}z)}.

(3.109)

Here,
k_{z}\in\mathbb{R}
is a free parameter and
k_{0}=+\sqrt{k_{\perp}^{2}+k_{z}^{2}}
. The master variables in this case are governed by the field equations

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{v}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}}{\partial t^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{v}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}}{\partial z^{2}}-k_{\perp}^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{v}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}}{\partial t^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}^{k_{\perp}k_{z}}}{\partial z^{2}}-k_{\perp}^{2}\Psi_{\mathrm{s}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.110)

and the inertial modes satisfy the normalization conditions

\displaystyle\langle H^{(\mathrm{p},\mathbf{k})},H^{(\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{3}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}),

(3.111a)

\displaystyle\langle H^{(\mathrm{p},\mathbf{k})},\overline{H^{(\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})}}\rangle_{\text{t}}=0,

(3.111b)

\displaystyle\langle\overline{H^{(\mathrm{p},\mathbf{k})}},\overline{H^{(\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})}}\rangle_{\text{t}}=-\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\,\delta^{3}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}),

(3.111c)

where
\mathrm{p}
again denotes the sector: either scalar or vector. Note that we have found the normalization using the inertial version of calculations of
Eqs. (
B.21
) and (
B.39
), which translate using the simple substitutions

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\mapsto k_{z},

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto k_{z}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto z,

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\mapsto t,

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle\mapsto\Psi,

(3.112)

as we have derived these in a covariant fashion.

3.3.4
Unruh modes

Up to this point in this chapter we have treated Rindler spacetime as an independent object: we have not made any reference to weather we are considering either
the left or right wedge. In general terms, the scalar mode in this
abstract
Rindler spacetime can be written as

v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}-\omega\lambda)}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(a^{-1}k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}),

(3.113)

expression which coincides with the right or left modes when evaluated using the respective Rindler coordinates. The gravitational perturbations can be written
using the scalar modes (
3.113
); we find the vector sector can be written as

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=0,

(3.114a)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{jl}\nabla^{\beta}\nabla^{l}v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.114b)

h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=0,

(3.114c)

and the scalar sector as

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g_{\alpha\beta}v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right),

(3.115a)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}=\frac{\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{j}v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.115b)

\displaystyle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g_{ij}v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right).

(3.115c)

Here the
\alpha,\beta
indices refer to the coordinates
\lambda
and
\xi
. Let us recall that the Bogoliubov transformations allow us to interpret the
scalar Rindler modes as distributions in Minkowski spacetime, with the help of the inertial scalar modes (
1.30
). This inspires one of two ways
of mapping the tensor modes to both wedges.
First we note that the forms of Eq. (
3.114
) and (
3.115
) are equivalent, due to the
covariance, if the indices
\alpha
and
\beta
are referring to the orbit on Minkowski spacetime (
t
,
z
coordinates). Then we can think of these
expressions as linear operators over the scalar modes
h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}[v_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}]
, and we have

\displaystyle V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}

\displaystyle=h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}[v^{\mathrm{R}}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}],

\displaystyle V^{(\mathrm{L},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}

\displaystyle=h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}[v^{\mathrm{L}}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}].

(3.116)

The second way of doing the extension is by simple analytical continuation. We write the right Rindler modes using the modes we introduced in the previous
section and the coordinate transformation of Eq. (
1.19
), i.e.

V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(\lambda,\xi,\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\equiv h^{(\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(\lambda,\xi,\mathbf{x}_{\perp}).

(3.117)

We have also shown that the right tensor Rindler modes depend on the scalar Rindler modes. Consequently, we can use the same procedure of analytic continuation
as described in Ref.
[
51
]
to define the right tensor Rindler modes as distributions on the entirety of Minkowski spacetime

V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(\lambda,\xi,\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\ \longrightarrow\ V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x}_{\perp},z),

(3.118)

which in turn allows us to define left Rindler modes using a wedge reflection

V^{(\mathrm{L},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x}_{\perp},z)\equiv\overline{V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega-\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(-t,\mathbf{x}_{\perp},-z)}.

(3.119)

We need to mention two important facts that are inherited by the tensor Rindler modes from the scalar case:

1.

the analytic extension of the Rindler modes requires that they are null in the contrary wedge, meaning that
V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x}_{\perp},z)=0
for all events with
z<-|t|
, and
V^{(\mathrm{L},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x}_{\perp},z)=0
for all events that satisfy
z>|t|
;

2.

neither left nor right Rindler modes are positive energy regarding inertial time
t
, as they have been defined from the boost generator
(\partial_{\lambda})^{a}
and not from the Killing vector
(\partial_{t})^{a}
, therefore, they are intrinsically related to the Physics of uniformly
accelerated reference frames.

However, we know it is possible to define a set of modes that are in fact positive energy with respect to inertial time using the scalar Unruh
modes (
1.47
), which we can generalize to the tensor case

W^{(1,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}=\frac{V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}+\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}\,\overline{V^{(\mathrm{L},\mathrm{p},\omega\,-\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}},

(3.120a)

W^{(2,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}=\frac{V^{(\mathrm{L},\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}+\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}\,\overline{V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{p},\omega\,-\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}},

(3.120b)

that along with their corresponding complex conjugates form a complete set of modes that can be used to describe gravitational perturbations as they satisfy

\displaystyle\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},W^{(\sigma^{\prime},\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\delta_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}),

(3.121a)

\displaystyle\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},\overline{W^{(\sigma^{\prime},\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}}\rangle_{\text{t}}=0,

(3.121b)

\displaystyle\langle\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}},\overline{W^{(\sigma^{\prime},\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}}\rangle_{\text{t}}=-\delta_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}).

(3.121c)

We can also write these explicitly as the vector sector

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=0,

(3.122a)

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\varepsilon_{jl}\nabla^{\beta}\nabla^{l}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.122b)

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=0,

(3.122c)

and the scalar one

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}[\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-(k_{\perp}^{2}/2)g_{\alpha\beta}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}],

(3.123a)

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}=\frac{\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{j}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(3.123b)

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}[\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+(k_{\perp}^{2}/2)g_{ij}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}],

(3.123c)

where we used the scalar Unruh modes (
1.47
).

3.4
Classical expansion of the gravitational perturbation

We can generalize the identity proposed by
[
65
]

[
65
]
to the gravitational case, like we
did for the electromagnetic field. Let us first consider a globally hyperbolic flat manifold
M
with its corresponding background metric
g_{ab}
and a
compactly supported stress-energy tensor
T_{ab}
that will act as a source of equation (
3.38
). Now, let
h_{ab}
be a
solution of the homogeneous field equation for the gravitational perturbation in the TT gauge; as the background is flat we see from
Eq. (
3.8
) that the homogeneous equation is given by

\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}h_{ab}=0.

(3.124)

Also, let us consider a Cauchy surface
\Sigma_{-}
that lies outside the causal future of the support of
T_{ab}
. We
can see that the retarded solution is null in
J^{-}(\supp T_{ab})
, in particular, the combination

ET_{ab}\coloneqq AT_{ab}-RT_{ab}

(3.125)

satisfies
ET_{ab}(x)=AT_{ab}(x)
for all events in
\Sigma_{-}
. In section
B.3
of the appendix we show how with these ingredients we can
construct the expression

\langle h,ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\iiiint_{M}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,T^{ab}\,\overline{h_{ab}},

(3.126)

which will prove to be useful ahead. Furthermore, as the modes are traceless we can write

\langle h,ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=\langle h,E\mathfrak{t}\rangle_{\text{t}}
, and therefore, we can use this expression to write the coefficients of the expansion of the gravitational waves
coming from a particle, in terms of Unruh modes.

3.4.1
Causal considerations and classical retarded field expansion

The reasoning here follows Refs.
[
22
,
53
]
. We consider two Cauchy surfaces
in the spacetime,
\Sigma_{+}
and
\Sigma_{-}
, where
\Sigma_{+}
is on the chronological future of
\Sigma_{-}
and neither of these surfaces are
crossed by the accelerated part of the worldline of the charge. A conformal illustration of this situation is presented on Fig.
3.1
.

Figure 3.1:
Penrose diagram of the moving mass.

The compactly supported trajectory is depicted here. The purple part is the inertial movement before the acceleration of the particle, shown in red
after which the particle becomes inertial again. The Cauchy surfaces lie outside of the causal past and future of this support, as depicted. Notice how
the trajectory leaves the RRW when the particle is inertial.

In general terms, the advanced and retarded solutions of the full field equation (
3.38
) are respectively given by

\displaystyle AT^{ab}(x)=\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\sqrt{-g(x^{\prime})}\mathchoice{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})T^{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}),

(3.127a)

\displaystyle RT^{ab}(x)=\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}\sqrt{-g(x^{\prime})}\mathchoice{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})T^{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}).

(3.127b)

Here
\mathchoice{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{adv}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})
and
\mathchoice{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}{{G_{\text{ret}}}^{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})
are the advanced and retarded bitensor
Green’s functions for the gravitational wave equation
[
81
]
. Given that we are working on a flat spacetime we can simply
define the regular part of the particular solution
ET^{ab}=AT^{ab}-RT^{ab}
, and considering our we have chosen our Cauchy hypersurfaces outside of
the support of the source we find that

\displaystyle AT^{ab}(x)=ET^{ab}(x)\quad\forall\ x\in\Sigma_{-},

(3.128a)

\displaystyle RT^{ab}(x)=-ET^{ab}(x)\quad\forall\ x\in\Sigma_{+}.

(3.128b)

This is due to the fact that in the future Cauchy surface only the current on the causal past of
\Sigma_{+}
contributes to the field, provided that the
trajectory of the charge does not cross this Cauchy surface. The reasoning is analogous for
\Sigma_{-}
.

The retarded solution can be expanded in terms of the Unruh modes

RT_{ab}=-\sum_{\sigma=1}^{2}\sum_{\mathrm{p}=\mathrm{s},\mathrm{v}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\left[\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}\,W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)+\text{c.c.}\right],

(3.129)

where we used Eq. (
3.125
) and we can apply the result of Eq. (
3.126
) to derive the
coefficients:

\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g(x)}\,T^{ab}(x)\,\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)},

(3.130)

which can be computed on the vector sector rather trivially

\langle W^{(1,\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=\langle W^{(2,\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=0,

(3.131)

as the only nonzero components of the stress-energy tensor do not couple with the vector sector modes, and the scalar sector can be computed quite
straightforwardly

\displaystyle\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g(x)}\,T^{ab}(x)\,\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g(x)}\,T^{\alpha\beta}(x)\,\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}(A^{(\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}+\mathcal{I}^{(\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}),

(3.132)

where
A^{(\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}
and
\mathcal{I}^{(\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}
correspond to the contributions by the accelerated and inertial
parts of the motion respectively. We find that these can be reduced to integrals over the RRW

\displaystyle A^{(1,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=m\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\tau\,u^{\alpha}(\tau)u^{\beta}(\tau)\,\overline{W^{(1,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}(\tau,0,0,0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}}\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\tau\,u^{\alpha}(\tau)u^{\beta}(\tau)\,\overline{V^{(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}(\tau,0,0,0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\kappa m}{k_{\perp}^{2}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}}\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\tau\left[-\omega^{2}\overline{v^{\mathrm{R}}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}}-a\partial_{\xi}\overline{v^{\mathrm{R}}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi}\overline{v^{\mathrm{R}}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}}\right]_{(\tau,0,0,0)}.

(3.133)

Using Eq. (
3.60
) and the scalar right Rindler modes (
1.37
) we get

\displaystyle A^{(1,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\kappa m}{k_{\perp}^{2}\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\tau\Bigg[\Bigg(-\omega^{2}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)-a\partial_{\xi}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)\Bigg)\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}-\omega\lambda)}\Bigg]_{(\tau,0,0,0)}.

(3.134)

we can now use the identity/recurrence relation
[
67
]

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathrm{K}_{\nu}(x)=-\frac{1}{2}[\mathrm{K}_{\nu-1}(x)+\mathrm{K}_{\nu+1}(x)]=-\frac{1}{2}[\mathrm{K}_{1-\nu}(x)+\mathrm{K}_{\nu+1}(x)],

(3.135)

to simplify the expressions a little while carrying out the integration:

\displaystyle A^{(1,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa m}{\pi^{2}k_{\perp}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\pi\omega/a}}{2a}}\,\frac{\sin(\omega T)}{\omega}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left\{\left(k_{\perp}^{2}-2\omega^{2}\right)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)+ak_{\perp}[\mathrm{K}_{1-\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)+\mathrm{K}_{1+\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)]\right\},

(3.136)

while for the second Unruh mode we get a very similar result (the details of the calculation are analogous)

A^{(2,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=\frac{\kappa m}{\pi^{2}k_{\perp}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{2a}}\frac{\sin(\omega T)}{\omega}\\
\times\left\{\left(k_{\perp}^{2}-2\omega^{2}\right)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)+ak_{\perp}[\mathrm{K}_{1-\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)+\mathrm{K}_{1+\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)]\right\}.

(3.137)

We deal with the inertial contributions in the appendix
B.4
.

Taking the limit of infinite acceleration time
T\to\infty
, using Eq. (
2.39
), we get

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\langle W^{(1,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\langle W^{(2,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}m\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\left[\mathrm{K}_{0}(k_{\perp}/a)+\frac{2a}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{K}_{1}(k_{\perp}/a)\right]\delta(\omega),

(3.138)

from where we see that ony zero-Rindler-energy modes (with
\omega=0
) contribute to building the field in this limit. We can also apply the recurrence
relation
[
61
]

\mathrm{K}_{\nu+1}(x)=\mathrm{K}_{\nu-1}(x)+\frac{2\nu}{x}\mathrm{K}_{\nu}(x),

(3.139)

to write the more simplified expression

\lim_{T\to\infty}\langle W^{(1,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=\lim_{T\to\infty}\langle W^{(2,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}m\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\delta(\omega).

(3.140)

These can be used to find the perturbation of the gravitational field generated by the accelerated mass in the radiation zone defined by
t>|z|
.

3.4.2
Building the perturbation from the expansion

We use the form of Eqs. (
3.122
) and (
3.123
) to describe the scalar Unruh modes
in the EDKU. First we find the nonzero Christoffel symbols of the second kind in this patch, using radar coordinates like those of Eq. (
1.19b
).
Then we can write the general forms of each component of the TT Unruh modes using the scalar Unruh modes that in this region are given by
Eq. (
2.47
). This procedure is straightforward but can lead to confusion due to the sheer amount of calculations involved. We can heavily
simplify it by applying the coefficients of Eq. (
3.138
) to the expansion (
3.129
) and use
W^{(1,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}=W^{(2,\mathrm{s},-\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}
, to find

RT_{ab}=-\frac{m\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\left[-\mathrm{i}\,W^{(2,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)+\mathrm{i}\,\overline{W^{(2,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)}\right]\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\delta(\omega)\\
=\frac{m\mathrm{i}\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\left[\,W^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)-\,\overline{W^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(x)}\right]\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a),

(3.141)

where only modes with zero-Rindler energy, i.e.,
\omega=0
, are considered; and then rewrite the transverse momentum vector in polar coordinates for the
nonzero components. As the position in the
xy
plane is
\mathbf{x}_{\perp}=(x_{\perp}\cos\varphi,x_{\perp}\sin\varphi)
, where
x_{\perp}=|\mathbf{x}_{\perp}|\geq 0
and
0\leq\varphi<2\pi
, we can write the transverse momentum as
\mathbf{k}_{\perp}=(k_{\perp}\cos\vartheta,k_{\perp}\sin\vartheta)
, with
k_{\perp}>0
and
\vartheta\in[0,2\pi)
, and as such,
\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}=k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}
, and
\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}=k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)
. Then, after applying the identity of Eq. (
2.48
), the nonzero components of
the Unruh mode can be written as

\displaystyle W_{\eta\eta}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)-\frac{a\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\right),

(3.142a)

\displaystyle W_{\zeta\zeta}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)-\frac{a\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\right),

(3.142b)

\displaystyle W_{\eta x}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=-\frac{\kappa\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{\sqrt{32\pi^{2}a}}\,\cos\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a),

(3.142c)

\displaystyle W_{\eta y}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=-\frac{\kappa\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{\sqrt{32\pi^{2}a}}\,\sin\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a),

(3.142d)

\displaystyle W_{xx}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\left(\frac{\cos^{2}\vartheta-\sin^{2}\vartheta}{2}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a),

(3.142e)

\displaystyle W_{xy}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\cos\vartheta\sin\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\,\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a),

(3.142f)

\displaystyle W_{yy}^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\left(\frac{\cos^{2}\vartheta-\sin^{2}\vartheta}{2}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\,\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a).

(3.142g)

Then we will find the angular integrals

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}u\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta=2\pi\mathrm{J}_{0}(u),

(3.143a)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}u\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\sin\vartheta\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta=2\pi\mathrm{i}\sin\varphi\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(u),

(3.143b)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}u\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\cos\vartheta\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta=2\pi\mathrm{i}\cos\varphi\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(u),

(3.143c)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}u\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\sin\vartheta\cos\vartheta\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta=-\pi\sin(2\varphi)\,\mathrm{J}_{2}(u),

(3.143d)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}u\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}(\cos^{2}\vartheta-\sin^{2}\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta=-2\pi\cos(2\varphi)\,\mathrm{J}_{2}(u),

(3.143e)

and their complex conjugates when computing Eq. (
3.141
). These results are discussed in
section
B.5
of the appendix.

We begin with the
\eta\eta
component which we can immediately see is the same as the
\zeta\zeta
component

\displaystyle RT_{\eta\eta}=RT_{\zeta\zeta}

\displaystyle=\frac{m\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\left(\mathrm{i}\,W^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\eta\eta}-\mathrm{i}\,\overline{W^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\eta\eta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{64\pi^{4}a^{2}}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg\{\mathrm{i}\,\left[\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)-\frac{a\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\mathrm{i}\,\left[-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}x_{\perp}\cos(\vartheta-\varphi)}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\overline{\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)}-\frac{a\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{k_{\perp}}\overline{\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)}\right)\right]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi m\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{64\pi^{4}a^{2}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{0}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)-\frac{a\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{k_{\perp}}\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\overline{\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)}-\frac{a\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}{k_{\perp}}\overline{\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)}\Bigg),

(3.144)

If we now use the definition of the Hankel functions of the second kind

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{J}_{0}(z)-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{Y}_{0}(z),

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{(2)}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{J}_{1}(z)-\mathrm{i}\mathrm{Y}_{1}(z),

(3.145)

we see that

\displaystyle RT_{\eta\eta}=RT_{\zeta\zeta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{4\pi a}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{0}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\mathrm{J}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)-\frac{2a}{k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}}\mathrm{J}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{4\pi a}\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{0}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\mathrm{J}_{2}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a),

(3.146)

where we applied
[
61
]

\mathrm{J}_{\nu+1}(x)=-\mathrm{J}_{\nu-1}(x)+\frac{2\nu}{x}\mathrm{J}_{\nu}(x).

(3.147)

The other components can be found in similar fashion (see section
B.6
of the appendix). These are

\displaystyle RT_{\eta x}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{4\pi a}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}\cos\varphi\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a),

(3.148a)

\displaystyle RT_{\eta y}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{4\pi a}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}\sin\varphi\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}k_{\perp}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a),

(3.148b)

\displaystyle RT_{xy}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{4\pi a}\sin(2\varphi)\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}{k_{\perp}}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\,\mathrm{J}_{2}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a),

(3.148c)

\displaystyle RT_{xx}=-RT_{yy}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}}{4\pi a}\cos(2\varphi)\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}{k_{\perp}}\,k_{\perp}\,\mathrm{J}_{0}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\eta}/a)\,\mathrm{J}_{2}(k_{\perp}x_{\perp})\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a),

(3.148d)

meaning we have to compute expressions of the type

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\vartheta\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(\alpha\vartheta)\,\mathrm{J}_{2}(\beta\vartheta)\,\mathrm{J}_{0}(\gamma\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta=\frac{[\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-\sqrt{(\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2})^{2}+4\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}]^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}\sqrt{(\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2})^{2}+4\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}}},

(3.149a)

and,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\vartheta\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(\alpha\vartheta)\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(\beta\vartheta)\,\mathrm{J}_{1}(\gamma\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}\vartheta\\
=\frac{\alpha^{2}+\beta^{2}+\gamma^{2}-\sqrt{(\alpha^{2}+\beta^{2}-\gamma^{2})^{2}+4\alpha^{2}\gamma^{2}}}{2\beta\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\alpha^{2}+\beta^{2}-\gamma^{2})^{2}+4\alpha^{2}\gamma^{2}}}+\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\right).

(3.149b)

These integrals are complicated and laborious, the explicit details in obtaining these results can be found in section
B.7
of the appendix. We
then have as a result

\displaystyle RT_{\eta\eta}=RT_{\zeta\zeta}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}a^{2}}{4\pi}\,\left[\frac{[a^{-2}-\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}/a^{2}+x_{\perp}^{2}-2a^{-1}\rho_{0}(x)]^{2}}{8a^{-2}\rho_{0}(x)}\right],

(3.150a)

\displaystyle RT_{\eta x}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}a^{2}}{4\pi}\left[x\left(\frac{a^{-2}+x_{\perp}^{2}+\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}/a^{2}-2\rho_{0}(x)/a}{2x_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2a\rho_{0}(x)}+1\right)\right],

(3.150b)

\displaystyle RT_{\eta y}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}a^{2}}{4\pi}\left[y\left(\frac{a^{-2}+x_{\perp}^{2}+\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}/a^{2}-2\rho_{0}(x)/a}{2x_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2a\rho_{0}(x)}+1\right)\right],

(3.150c)

\displaystyle RT_{xy}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}a^{2}}{4\pi}\left[xy\,\frac{[a^{-2}+\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}/a^{2}-x_{\perp}^{2}-2a^{-1}\rho_{0}(x)]^{2}}{4x_{\perp}^{4}\rho_{0}(x)}\right],

(3.150d)

\displaystyle RT_{xx}=-RT_{yy}=\frac{m\kappa^{2}a^{2}}{4\pi}\left[(x^{2}-y^{2})\,\frac{[a^{-2}+\mathrm{e}^{2a\eta}/a^{2}-x_{\perp}^{2}-2a^{-1}\rho_{0}(x)]^{2}}{8x_{\perp}^{4}\rho_{0}(x)}\right].

(3.150e)

As far as we know these expressions for the gravitational wave coming from a
single
uniformly accelerated charge were unreported in the literature.

3.5
Quantum expansion of the field

Let us now study the quantized gravitational waves by promoting the gravitational perturbations and the associated conjugated momenta to operator status and
imposing the equal time canonical commutation relations

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x}),\hat{h}_{cd}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})]=0,

(3.151a)

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x}),n_{e}\hat{\pi}^{ecd}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})]=-\mathrm{i}\delta^{c}_{(a}\delta^{d}_{d)}\delta^{3}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.151b)

\displaystyle[\hat{\pi}^{fab}(t,\mathbf{x}),\hat{\pi}^{ecd}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})]=0,

(3.151c)

where
n^{a}=(\partial_{t})^{a}
is the normal future-oriented vector thats orthogonal to the Cauchy surface defined by the condition of constant time. We can
consider a compactly supported stress-energy tensor
T_{ab}
like we did in previous sections and recover the physical solution through a limiting process
(see Sect.
3.2
); and two Cauchy surfaces:
\Sigma_{+}
lying outside of the causal past of the support of the stress-energy tensor (also
called from now on the asymptotic future), and
\Sigma_{-}
lying on outside of the causal future of the support of the stress-energy tensor (asymptotic past),
like we did on section
3.4.1
(see also Fig.
3.1
). We proceed similarly to
the electromagnetic analysis and write the quantized gravitational perturbation in two different forms, each depending on the perspective of an observer
situated on the Cauchy surfaces: for an observer on the past we have

\hat{h}_{ab}(x)=\hat{h}^{\text{out}}_{ab}(x)+A\mathfrak{t}_{ab}(x)\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.152)

where
\hat{h}^{\text{out}}_{ab}
is the free field determined form the homogeneous field equation in the TT
gauge of Eq. (
3.124
), and
A\mathfrak{t}_{ab}
is the advanced particular solution of Eq. (
3.38
).
Given a choice of Minkowski positive-energy modes
v_{ab}^{(j)}(x)
, characterized by an appropriate set of numbers
j\in\mathfrak{J}
, we can expand the
homogeneous field as

\hat{h}^{\text{out}}_{ab}=\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{J}}\left[v_{ab}^{(j)}\,\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{v^{(j)}})+\overline{v_{ab}^{(j)}}\,\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(v^{(j)})\right].

(3.153)

Then there exists a state
|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle
(called the out vacuum) defined from the action of the annihilation operators over it:
\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{v^{(j)}})|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle=0
for all
j\in\mathfrak{J}
. We can see that as
A\mathfrak{t}_{ab}(x)=0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus J^{-}(\supp\mathfrak{t}_{ab})
, which means we can interpret the state
|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle
as the vacuum seem by inertial
observers on the asymptotic future.

Analogously, we can also write the quantized field as

\hat{h}_{ab}(x)=\hat{h}^{\text{in}}_{ab}(x)+R\mathfrak{t}_{ab}(x)\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.154)

where
R\mathfrak{t}_{ab}(x)
is the classical retarded field and
\hat{h}^{\text{in}}_{ab}
is a homogeneous solution of the linearized Einstein field equations,
which can be expanded using another set of positive energy modes
u_{ab}^{(k)}(x)
, characterized by their own set of numbers
k\in\mathfrak{K}
, this is,

\hat{h}^{\text{in}}_{ab}=\sum_{k\in\mathfrak{K}}\left[u_{ab}^{(k)}\,\hat{a}_{\text{in}}(\overline{u^{(k)}})+\overline{u_{ab}^{(k)}}\,\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{in}}(u^{(k)})\right].

(3.155)

We can define the vacuum state
|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{in}}\rangle
by the action of the annihilation operator over it, just like the previous case:
\hat{a}_{\text{in}}(\overline{u^{(k)}})|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{in}}\rangle=0
for all
k\in\mathfrak{K}
. Following the same reasoning as above we can see
that
R\mathfrak{t}_{ab}(x)=0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus J^{+}(\supp\mathfrak{t}_{ab})
implies that
|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{in}}\rangle
can be interpreted as the
vacuum seen by observers in the asymptotic past.

3.5.1
The S matrix

The Fock spaces for the asymptotic past and future are constructed form the consecutive application of the creation operators
\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{in}}(u^{(k)})
and
\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(v^{(j)})
over each of their respective associated vacua
|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{in}}\rangle
and
|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle
. The S matrix
[
64
]

\hat{S}_{\text{full}}\coloneqq\exp(\mathrm{i}I_{\text{int}}[\hat{h}^{\text{out}},\mathfrak{t}_{\text{full}}])\equiv\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,\hat{h}_{ab}^{\text{out}}\,\mathfrak{t}_{\text{full}}^{ab}\right),

(3.156)

is constructed to connect both of these. It relates the vacua by the simple equation

|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{in}}\rangle=\hat{S}_{\text{full}}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle.

(3.157)

In our case the modes are traceless and transverse, meaning the S matrix simplifies a great deal

\hat{S}_{\text{full}}=\exp(\mathrm{i}I_{\text{int}}[\hat{h}^{\text{out}},\mathfrak{t}_{\text{full}}])=\exp(\mathrm{i}I_{\text{int}}[\hat{h}^{\text{out}},T_{\text{full}}]),

(3.158)

and the stress-energy tensor is separated as in Eq. (
3.33
):
T^{\text{full}}_{ab}
, which we can rewrite as

T^{\text{full}}_{ab}=T^{F}_{ab}+T_{ab},

(3.159)

where we reabsorb all contributions of the accelerating agent into the stress-energy tensor
T^{F}_{ab}
, and
T_{ab}
is the one we have been
using and defined in Eq. (
3.34
) using the trajectory (
3.35
). The Zassenhaus
formula (
2.66
) allows us to write

\hat{S}_{\text{full}}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta}\hat{S}_{F}\hat{S},

(3.160)

where

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{F}

\displaystyle=\exp(\mathrm{i}I_{\text{int}}[\hat{h}^{\text{out}},T_{F}]),

\displaystyle\hat{S}

\displaystyle=\exp(\mathrm{i}I_{\text{int}}[\hat{h}^{\text{out}},T]),

(3.161)

and we defined

\Theta=\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\Delta^{abcd}(x,x^{\prime})T^{F}_{ab}(x)T_{ab}(x^{\prime})\sqrt{-g(x)}\sqrt{-g(x^{\prime})}\,\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\mathrm{d}^{4}x^{\prime}.

(3.162)

Here, the two-point function
\Delta^{abcd}
is defined from the commutator
[\hat{h}^{\text{out}}_{ab}(x),\hat{h}^{\text{out}}_{cd}(x^{\prime})]=\mathrm{i}\Delta^{abcd}(x,x^{\prime})\hat{\mathbb{1}}
.

We can write the operator
\hat{S}
explicitly using Unruh modes to expand the out-field. In this case
j=(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})
,
\mathfrak{J}=\{1,2\}\times\{\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s}\}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and the annihilation and creation operators are found directly from the inner
product thanks to the normalization of the modes:

\displaystyle\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})

\displaystyle=\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},\hat{h}\rangle_{\text{t}},

\displaystyle\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})})

\displaystyle=\langle\hat{h},W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}.

(3.163)

We have

\hat{S}=\exp\Bigg[\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,T^{ab}\\
\times\left(\sum_{\sigma,\mathrm{p}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\left[W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})+\text{H.c.}\right]\right)\Bigg].

(3.164)

By reordering the integrals we can identify the inner products of Eq. (
3.130
), which allow us to define the creation operator
associated to the positive energy part of the expansion

\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)\coloneqq\sum_{\sigma,\mathrm{p}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}\,\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}),

(3.165)

and the corresponding annihilation operator associated to the negative energy part

\displaystyle\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{KET})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\sigma,\mathrm{p}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\overline{\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}}\,\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})})

\displaystyle\equiv[\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)]^{\dagger},

(3.166)

so we can rewrite Eq. (
3.164
) in a more compact fashion

\hat{S}=\exp[\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{KET})-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)].

(3.167)
