This is advantageous to further reduce the expression by application of the Zassenhaus formula, as we will see now. First we define the modulus of the positive
energy part of the classical expansion:

\|KET\|^{2}\coloneqq\langle KET,KET\rangle_{\text{t}}=\sum_{\sigma,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\omega\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\,|\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}|^{2},

(3.168)

to then find the commutators of the creation and annihilation operators associated to the positive energy parts:
[\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{KET}),\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)]
. With the aid of the canonical commutation relation

[\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}}),\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(W^{(\sigma^{\prime},\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})})]\\
=[\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},\hat{h}\rangle_{\text{t}},\langle\hat{h},W^{(\sigma^{\prime},\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}\rangle_{\text{t}}]=\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},W^{(\sigma^{\prime},\mathrm{p}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}\rangle_{\text{t}}\hat{\mathbb{1}}\\
=\delta_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathrm{p}\mathrm{p}^{\prime}}\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.169)

we see this commutator reduces to

[\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{KET}),\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)]=\|KET\|^{2}\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.170)

which commutes with everything. Therefore, we can apply the Zassenhaus formula of Eq. (
2.66
), to see that

\hat{S}=\mathrm{e}^{-\|KET\|^{2}/2}\mathrm{e}^{-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}\mathrm{e}^{\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{KET})},

(3.171)

which imples the past vacuum is given by the more simple form

|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{in}}\rangle=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta}\hat{S}_{F}\mathrm{e}^{-\|KET\|^{2}/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle.

(3.172)

We can use the expression of Eq. (
3.172
) to show the in vacuum is a coherent state of the out annihilation operator associated to
Unruh modes. This reads explicitly

\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})\hat{S}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle=\mathrm{e}^{-\|KET\|^{2}/2}\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})\,\mathrm{e}^{-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle\\
=\mathrm{e}^{-\|KET\|^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}[\mathrm{e}^{\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})\mathrm{e}^{-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}]|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle.

(3.173)

The Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula (
2.72
) can be applied here. For this we need the canonical commutation relation

[\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET),\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})]=-\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.174)

and thus

\mathrm{e}^{\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})\mathrm{e}^{-\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)}=\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})-\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}\hat{\mathbb{1}},

(3.175)

from where we can see that

\hat{a}_{\text{out}}(\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}})\hat{S}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle=-\langle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{p},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},ET\rangle_{\text{t}}\hat{S}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle,

(3.176)

i.e., the out vacuum evolved by the interaction of the field with the particle is a multimode coherent state of the annihilation operator associated to an
arbitrary Unruh mode.

3.5.2
Zero-Rindler-energy gravitons

Considering now the case when the acceleration time of the particle is infinite, we find the coefficients of the expansion will be given by our result of
Eq. (
3.140
), meaning only zero-Rindler-energy modes will contribute to the description, and the creation operator can be
written as

\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(KET)=-\frac{\mathrm{i}m\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(W^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}).

(3.177)

Now, we can see that, as for different values of the transverse momentum
\mathbb{k}_{\perp}
the creation operators commute between themselves, and we can
write as the independent tensor product

\hat{S}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle=\bigotimes_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m^{2}\kappa^{2}}{16\pi^{3}a}T_{\mathrm{tot}}[\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)]^{2}\right\}\\
\times\exp{\left[\frac{\mathrm{i}m\kappa}{\sqrt{8\pi^{2}a}}\mathrm{K}_{2}(k_{\perp}/a)\hat{a}^{\dagger}_{\text{out}}(W^{(2,\mathrm{s},0\mathbf{k}_{\perp})})\right]}|0^{\text{M}}_{\text{out}}\rangle,

(3.178)

where we now take care in not considering the value
\mathbf{k}_{\perp}=0
. Here we see explicitly that only zero-Rindler-energy gravitons contribute into
building this evolved state.

3.6
Partial discussion

We were successful in analyzing the radiation content stemming from the uniformly accelerated mass without considering the contribution due to the forcing
agent. To do this we defined a new set of gravitational Unruh modes analogous to those of the scalar and electrodynamical theories, by taking advantage of the
symmetries of the background geometry.

We showed how to obtain the expansion according to an inertial observer, using the quantum numbers seen by an accelerated one, in both the quantum and classical
contexts. Moreover, we showed a novel result in finding the perturbation of spacetime due only to the influence of the accelerated particle where only
zero-Rindler-energy modes contribute into building this expansion.

After this we connected the observations of inertial observers in the asymptotic past and future by describing how the interaction of the gravitational field
and the accelerating particle modifies the particle content of the field, such that the future observer sees the past vacuum as a multimode coherent
superposition of particles. Zero-Rindler-energy particles take the spotlight when the acceleration time is infinite, as they construct the entirety of the
multimode state.

– Chapter 4 –
Final Discussion

In this thesis we have studied the radiation coming from uniformly accelerated particles for both the electromagnetic and (weak) gravitational fields, in the
classical and quantum contexts, from the perspective of inertial and (co)accelerated observers. This survey took advantage of the dependance of the quantization
on the observer and the tools of Quantum Field Theory in Curved Spacetimes, to explore the influence of the acceleration on the evolution of the field when
comparing the state between the past and future. Our main tool to study this phenomena was our generalization of Unruh modes to be tensor- and vector-valued
functions.

This generalization of Unruh modes is what allows us to connect both perspectives for each field studied, as these use the numbers and quantities measured by
the accelerated observer to describe the physics of inertial ones, they provide a bridge between both of these frameworks and clearly show how the accelerated
particle influences the radiation seen by inertial observers. Even though the process of obtaining these was not easy, specially for the tensor case, we can
observe that the planar symmetry of the systems we studied, this being the
xy
plane, played heavily in our favor, simplifying the calculations a great deal.

One of the more remarkable findings of this work is the confirmation of how the purely quantum Unruh effect interplays and manifests itself in the classical
realm as Larmor radiation. This was demonstrated more spectacularly in the electrodynamic case, where we found that the expectation values of the evolved fields
coincide with their classical counterparts, from where the derivation of Larmor’s formula is a direct affair.

Zero-Rindler-energy particles are also surprising. A particle with no energy seems to make no sense for any kind of observer. Moreover, if we consider them as
simple waves, a zero energy perturbation would represent a static modification of the field: “a wave that does not oscillate in time,” a completely
counterintuitive and unorthodox concept. However, we know non-inertial observers do not observe the world as we are used to, we know physical theories heavily
rely on the privilege of the inertial observer, and to them these zero-Rindler-energy particles (or modes) do not appear as having zero energy,
but they
are formed as a coherent superposition of what they see as their natural oscillation states (plane waves)
, and therefore, carry an infinitely many distinct
energies (to the point when we cannot define a single inertial energy value for these modes). More surprisingly, it is only these particles/modes who build the
field in the case when the particle is accelerated for an infinite amount of time, in other words they have been hiding in plain sight, as the case of infinite
acceleration time is the most widely studied for being the simplest possible account of uniformly accelerated particles. This even qualms the complaints by
Pauli and Feynman, mentioned in the introduction, as the zero-Rindler-energy modes will not be detectable by the coaccelerated observer: they will not excite a
detector carried by them, further validating the claims by Rohrlich and Boulware that radiation is not a covariant concept.

This entire analysis clarifies what zero-Rindler-energy modes are, what part do they play in the description of radiation, and provides an explanation of how
the Unruh effect is responsible for the classical detectability of radiation. It was in the process of obtaining these results that the connection between the
asymptotic past and future was heavily relied upon, particularly in using the S matrix for the quantum study.

As future work we can mention that it would be interesting to study how the initial state of the fields affect the radiation content seen by future observers,
or how the excitation rates of Unruh-DeWitt detectors compare when they are infinitely distant form the source and see if it is possible to give a definition of
radiation and under what conditions this definition holds.

Appendix A
Explicit calculations for the electromagnetic part

A.1
Derivation of the identity used to find the coefficients of the expansion

Let
\tilde{A}_{a}
be a solution of the homogeneous field equation for the electromagnetic potential
(
2.11
). We can compute the integral

\iiiint_{M}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}j^{a}=\iiiint_{J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}j^{a},

(A.1)

where
J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})
denotes the causal future of
\Sigma_{-}
. The
advanced solution (
2.33a
) satisfies
\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}Aj_{a}=j_{a}
in this region, meaning we can replace the current inside the integral

\iiiint_{M}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}j^{a}=\iiiint_{J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}(\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}Aj^{a}).

(A.2)

From the properties of the covariant derivative and from
(
2.11
), we find the relation

\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}(\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}Aj^{a})=\nabla_{b}(\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\nabla}}}{}^{b}Aj^{a}).

(A.3)

We can reconstruct this structure using the generalized Klein-Gordon inner
product (
2.12
). For our case in study:

\Xi^{b}[\tilde{A},Aj]=2\mathrm{i}\nabla_{a}(\overline{\tilde{A}^{[a}}Aj^{b]})-\mathrm{i}\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\nabla}}}{}^{b}Aj^{a},

(A.4)

result that can be plugged in Eq. (
A.3
). If also take advantage
of the skew-symmetry of the last term and the properties of the covariant
derivatives, we find

\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}(\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}Aj^{a})=\mathrm{i}\nabla_{a}\Xi^{a}[\tilde{A},Aj]+2R_{ab}\overline{\tilde{A}^{a}}Aj^{b},

(A.5)

where
R_{ab}\coloneqq R^{c}{}_{acb}
is the Ricci curvature tensor of the
spacetime. This is enough to compute the integral (
A.1
). Using
Gauss’ surface integral identity we find that the covariant derivative of the
current
\Xi^{a}
is simply a border term where the only contribution is by the
integration over the Cauchy surface, thus we can identify it with the internal
product (
2.12
) and write

\iiiint_{M}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}j^{a}=2\iiiint_{J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,R_{ab}\tilde{A}^{a}Aj^{b}+\mathrm{i}\langle\tilde{A},Aj\rangle_{\text{v}}.

(A.6)

We now remember that we have already assumed that our study has been restricted
to Ricci-flat globally hyperbolic spacetimes, as this is a requirement for Eq.
(
2.10
) to hold, thus

\langle\tilde{A},Aj\rangle_{\text{v}}=-\mathrm{i}\iiiint_{M}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\overline{\tilde{A}_{a}}j^{a}.

(A.7)

Now, given our choice of Cauchy surface for the calculation of the generalized
Klein-Gordon inner product, by using Eq. (
2.34
) we find Eq. (
2.36
)

A.2
Finding the coefficients of the expansion

We separate
\langle W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)},Ej\rangle_{\text{v}}=A_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)}+\mathcal{I}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)}
.
We have:

\displaystyle A_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{1(2)}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})j^{\lambda}(x)\,\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{1(2)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}^{-1}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})\left(q\delta(\xi)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\theta(T-|\lambda|)\right)\overline{V_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{R(2)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}q}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right]\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\mathrm{i}q}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right]\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\frac{\sin(\omega T)}{\omega},

(A.8a)

\displaystyle A_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{1(G)}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})j^{\lambda}(x)\,\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{1(G)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}^{-1}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})\left(q\delta(\xi)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\theta(T-|\lambda|)\right)\overline{V_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{R(G)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}qk_{\perp}^{-1}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right](\mathrm{i}\omega)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{2q\omega k_{\perp}^{-1}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right]\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\frac{\sin(\omega T)}{\omega},

(A.8b)

\displaystyle A_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{2(2)}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})j^{\lambda}(x)\,\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{1(2)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})\left(q\delta(\xi)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\theta(T-|\lambda|)\right)V_{\omega\,-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{R(2)}{}_{\lambda}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}q\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right]\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\mathrm{i}q\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right]\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\frac{\sin(\omega T)}{\omega},

(A.8c)

\displaystyle A_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{2(G)}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})j^{\lambda}(x)\,\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{2(G)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x(\mathrm{e}^{2a\xi})\left(q\delta(\xi)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\theta(T-|\lambda|)\right)\overline{V_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{R(G)}{}_{\lambda}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}qk_{\perp}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right](-\mathrm{i}\omega)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\int_{-T}^{T}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\omega\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2q\omega k_{\perp}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\omega/a}}{\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\omega/a}}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}}\right]\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}/a)\,\frac{\sin(\omega T)}{\omega},

(A.8d)

A.3
Inertial motion contributions

We can write the inertial contributions as:

\mathcal{I}^{\sigma(\kappa)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\equiv-\mathrm{i}q\left[\cosh(aT)\mathcal{A}^{\sigma(\kappa)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+\sinh(aT)\mathcal{B}^{\sigma(\kappa)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right],

(A.9)

where we have separated

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\sigma(\kappa)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\coloneqq\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}z\left[\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)}{}_{t}(t,0,0,z)}\Delta_{-}(z,t)+\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)}{}_{t}(-t,0,0,z)}\Delta_{+}(z,-t)\right],

(A.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\sigma(\kappa)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\coloneqq\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}z\left[\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)}{}_{z}(t,0,0,z)}\Delta_{-}(z,t)-\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(\kappa)}{}_{z}(-t,0,0,z)}\Delta_{+}(z,-t)\right],

(A.11)

and
\Delta_{\pm}(z,t)
is defined as in Eq. (
2.17
). As we can safely eliminate the mode with
\kappa=G
we will only concern ourselves
with
\kappa=2
We can first see that
\Delta_{+}(z,-t)=\Delta_{-}(z,t)
to rewrite
Eqs. (
A.10
) and (
A.11
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}z\left[\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(2)}{}_{t}(t,0,0,z)}+\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(2)*}{}_{t}(-t,0,0,z)}\right]\Delta_{-}(z,t),

(A.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}z\left[\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(2)}{}_{z}(t,0,0,z)}-\overline{W_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{\sigma(2)}{}_{z}(-t,0,0,z)}\right]\Delta_{-}(z,t).

(A.13)

Next, we can use the integral form of the scalar Unruh modes (
1.50
) to find integral expressions for the components of the
vector Unruh modes we need [see Eq. (
2.27b
)]:

\displaystyle\overline{W^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}{}_{t}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}}{(2\pi)^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\sinh\vartheta\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)],

(A.14)

\displaystyle\overline{W^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}{}_{z}}=+\frac{\mathrm{i}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}}{(2\pi)^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\cosh\vartheta\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)].

(A.15)

Now, by applying Eqs. (
A.14
) and (
A.15
) into Eqs. (
A.12
) and (
A.13
) we find that

\mathcal{A}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}z\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\sinh\vartheta\\
\times\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}z\sinh\vartheta}\cos(k_{\perp}t\cosh\vartheta)\Delta_{-}(z,t),

(A.16)

and,

\mathcal{B}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}z\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\cosh\vartheta\\
\times\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}z\sinh\vartheta}\sin(k_{\perp}t\cosh\vartheta)\Delta_{-}(z,t).

(A.17)

The integrals in the
z
variable in the above expressions can be computed immediately yielding

\mathcal{A}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}[(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega+k_{\perp}\sech(aT)\sinh\vartheta]/a}\\
\times\sinh\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\cos(k_{\perp}t\cosh\vartheta),

(A.18)

and

\mathcal{B}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}[(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega+k_{\perp}\sech(aT)\sinh\vartheta]/a}\\
\times\cosh\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\sin(k_{\perp}t\cosh\vartheta).

(A.19)

It is convenient to define

\displaystyle f^{A}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)\equiv\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\cos(k_{\perp}t\cosh\vartheta),

(A.20a)

\displaystyle f^{B}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)\equiv\int_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}^{L}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\sin(k_{\perp}t\cosh\vartheta),

(A.20b)

such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}[(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega+k_{\perp}\sech(aT)\sinh\vartheta]/a}f^{A}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)\sinh\vartheta,

(A.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\sigma(2)}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}[(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega+k_{\perp}\sech(aT)\sinh\vartheta]/a}f^{B}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)\cosh\vartheta.

(A.22)

From this we realize that to study the behavior of the physical current (recovered when
L\to\infty
) and of the infinite acceleration proper-time (i.e.,

T\to\infty
), it is enough to analyze these
f_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)
.

Direct integration shows that

f^{A}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\sinh\vartheta\tanh(aT)}{k_{\perp}[\sinh^{2}\vartheta\tanh^{2}(aT)-\cosh^{2}\vartheta]}\\
\times\left\{\mathrm{e}^{ik_{\perp}L\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\left[\cos(k_{\perp}L\cosh\vartheta)+\frac{\mathrm{i}\coth\vartheta}{\tanh(aT)}\sin(k_{\perp}L\cosh\vartheta)\right]\right.\\
+\mathrm{e}^{i(k_{\perp}/a)\sinh(aT)\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\\
\times\left.\left[\cos\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)+\frac{\mathrm{i}\coth\vartheta}{\tanh(aT)}\sin\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)\right]\right\},

(A.23)

and,

f^{B}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\sinh\vartheta\tanh(aT)}{k_{\perp}[\sinh^{2}\vartheta\tanh^{2}(aT)-\cosh^{2}\vartheta]}\\
\times\left\{\mathrm{e}^{ik_{\perp}L\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\left[\sin(k_{\perp}L\cosh\vartheta)-\frac{\mathrm{i}\coth\vartheta}{\tanh(aT)}\cos(k_{\perp}L\cosh\vartheta)\right]\right.\\
+\mathrm{e}^{i(k_{\perp}/a)\sinh(aT)\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\\
\times\left.\left[\sin\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)-\frac{\mathrm{i}\coth\vartheta}{\tanh(aT)}\cos\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)\right]\right\}.

(A.24)

We can use a corollary of Eq. (
2.39
)

\lim_{L\to\infty}\cos(\Omega L)=\lim_{L\to\infty}\sin(\Omega L)=\pi\Omega\delta(\Omega)\overset{!}{=}0,

(A.25)

to show this implies that

\lim_{L\to\infty}f^{A}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\sinh\vartheta\tanh(aT)}{k_{\perp}[\sinh^{2}\vartheta\tanh^{2}(aT)-\cosh^{2}\vartheta]}\mathrm{e}^{i(k_{\perp}/a)\sinh(aT)\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\\
\times\left[\cos\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)+\frac{\mathrm{i}\coth\vartheta}{\tanh(aT)}\sin\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)\right],

(A.26)

and

\lim_{L\to\infty}f^{B}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)=\frac{\mathrm{i}\sinh\vartheta\tanh(aT)}{k_{\perp}[\sinh^{2}\vartheta\tanh^{2}(aT)-\cosh^{2}\vartheta]}\mathrm{e}^{i(k_{\perp}/a)\sinh(aT)\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta}\\
\times\left[\sin\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)-\frac{\mathrm{i}\coth\vartheta}{\tanh(aT)}\cos\!\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{a}\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\right)\right].

(A.27)

The same arguments can be used in the limit
T\to\infty
(as
\sinh
is a strictly growing function) to see that

\lim_{T\to\infty}\lim_{L\to\infty}f^{A}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)=\lim_{T\to\infty}\lim_{L\to\infty}f^{B}_{a}(\vartheta,L,T)=0,

(A.28)

which, by using Eq. (
A.9
), (
A.21
), and (
A.22
) implies the result of Eq. (
2.38
).

Appendix B
Explicit calculations for the gravitational part

B.1
Proof the gauge-transformed modes are traceless and transverse

We can explicitly show these are in the TT gauge. The traces are both nil:

g^{ab}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}=g^{\alpha\beta}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}+g^{ij}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=g^{\alpha\beta}(0)+g^{ij}(0)=0,

(B.1)

and

g^{ab}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}=g^{\alpha\beta}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}+g^{ij}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}\\
=\left(\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}-k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\right)\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}(g^{ij}\mathbb{S}_{ij}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}})=(0)\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}(0)=0;

(B.2)

while the covariant divergence of the vector sector is given by

\nabla^{a}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{a\beta}=\nabla^{\alpha}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}+\nabla^{i}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{i\beta}=0+\nabla^{i}\left(\epsilon_{\beta\gamma}\nabla^{\gamma}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{V}_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)=0,

(B.3)

for the orbit components and,

\nabla^{a}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{aj}=\nabla^{\alpha}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}+\nabla^{i}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}=\nabla^{\alpha}\left(\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{V}_{j}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)+0=(0)\mathbb{V}_{j}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=0.

(B.4)

for the other ones. The scalar sector

\nabla^{a}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{a\beta}=\nabla^{\alpha}\left(\left[\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}g_{\alpha\beta}\right]\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)+\nabla^{i}\left(-\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{i}\right)\\
=\left[\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\right]\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}(k_{\perp}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}})=0,

(B.5)

and,

\nabla^{a}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{aj}=\nabla^{\alpha}\left(-\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{j}\right)+\nabla^{i}\left(k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}_{ij}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)\\
=-\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}(k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}})\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{j}+k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\left(\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\mathbb{S}_{j}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)=0,

(B.6)

as we wanted to show.

B.2
Normalization of the gravitational TT Unruh modes

To normalize, we need the inverse metric perturbations of the vector and scalar modes from Eqs. (
3.100
) and
(
3.101
), which are respectively given by

\displaystyle h_{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha\beta}=0,

(B.7a)

\displaystyle h_{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\,\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{j},

(B.7b)

\displaystyle h_{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{ij}=0,

(B.7c)

and,

\displaystyle h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha\beta}=\left(\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}g^{\alpha\beta}\right)\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(B.8a)

\displaystyle h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}=-\frac{k_{\perp}}{2}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{j},

(B.8b)

\displaystyle h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{ij}=k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}\mathbb{S}^{ij}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}.

(B.8c)

Note we have included the negative sign in the
h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}
components as it is dependent on a pseudo-tensor which changes
signs when raising its indices. We can start by computing the normalization current (
3.94
) between the scalar and vector
sectors, however we are only interested in the
\lambda
component, so we use Greek indices specifically for this calculation in order to avoid derivatives in
the
xy
-plane that are not relevant in this setting

W_{\gamma}[h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}]=0+2\left(\overline{h_{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha i}}\nabla_{\gamma}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{\alpha i}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{\alpha i}\nabla_{\gamma}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha i}}\right)+0\\
=-k^{\prime}_{\perp}\left(-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\gamma}[\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}]-\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}\nabla_{\gamma}[\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}]\right)\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}.

(B.9)

We see that the inner product will then depend on the integral

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{k^{\prime}_{\perp}}\left(k_{x}k_{y}^{\prime}-k_{x}^{\prime}k_{y}\right)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi^{2}\mathrm{i}}{k^{\prime}_{\perp}}\left(k_{x}k_{y}^{\prime}-k_{x}^{\prime}k_{y}\right)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}),

(B.10)

which can be further simplified by noting that, thanks to the Dirac delta, the only possibility that these are nonzero occurs when
\mathbf{k}_{\perp}=\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}
but due to the structure of the coefficients we find

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}=-\frac{4\pi^{2}\mathrm{i}}{k_{\perp}}\left(k_{x}k_{y}-k_{x}k_{y}\right)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})\equiv 0,

(B.11)

This implies that the vector sector is orthogonal to the scalar sector
, i.e.,
\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=0
.

The vector sector can be normalized as follows. We compute the current using the modes of Eqs. (
3.100
)
and (
B.7
)

W_{\gamma}[h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}]=0+2\left(\overline{h_{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha i}}\nabla_{\gamma}h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{\alpha i}-h_{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{\alpha i}\nabla_{\gamma}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha i}}\right)+0\\
=-2\left([\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}]\nabla_{\gamma}[\epsilon_{\alpha\delta}\nabla^{\delta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}]-[\epsilon^{\alpha\delta}\nabla_{\delta}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}]\nabla_{\gamma}[\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}]\right)\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}.

(B.12)

And now take advantage of the fact that the Levi-Civita pseudo-tensor is covariantly constant, so they commute with the covariant derivative. We also use the
contraction
\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\alpha\delta}=\delta^{\beta}_{\delta}
to reduce this to the form

W_{\gamma}[h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}]=-2\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\gamma}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}\nabla_{\gamma}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}.

(B.13)

We can compute
\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}
using the definition from
Eq. (
3.93
). We find

\displaystyle\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\kappa^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\,\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i},

(B.14)

which we can analyze separately. For the integral over the plane

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\overline{\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{V}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}=(k_{x}k_{x}^{\prime}+k_{y}k_{y}^{\prime})\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\!\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}=4\pi^{2}k_{\perp}^{2}\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}),

(B.15)

where we dropped the prime on the factor before the delta as its the only contributing term. Then

\displaystyle\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}4\pi^{2}\kappa^{-2}k_{\perp}^{2}\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right).

(B.16)

Again we only consider the terms with
\mathbf{k}_{\perp}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}
outside of the Dirac delta, as in the other case we know the result is zero. Let us analyze the integrand inside the parenthesis. Expanding the contraction:

\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\\
=\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\\
+\nabla_{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}.

(B.17)

We can replace the terms where
\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}
appears on the
right-hand side using the field equation for the master variable:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=k_{\perp}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\xi}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}

\displaystyle=k_{\perp}^{2}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\nabla_{\xi}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}},

(B.18)

and take advantage of the commutativity of the covariant derivatives in Rindler
spacetime to show directly that

\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\\
=k_{\perp}^{2}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right)\\
+\nabla_{\xi}\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right).

(B.19)

The last term inside the covariant derivative yields a border term. We can see
this simply computing the covariant derivative explicitly

\nabla_{\xi}\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right)=\partial_{\xi}\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right),

(B.20)

and noticing that the terms where Christoffel symbol appear cancel out equivalently. We can replace this in
Eq. (
B.16
) and disregard the total derivative

\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}\\
=-\mathrm{i}4\pi^{2}\kappa^{-2}k_{\perp}^{4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right)\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}).

(B.21)

Replacing the form of the vector sector master variable given in
Eq. (
3.76
) we have

\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\\
=\overline{V_{\omega k_{\perp}}}V_{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}[i(\omega+\omega^{\prime})]\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\lambda}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega^{\prime}/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a).

(B.22)

We can now use the
identity
[
19
]

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega^{\prime}/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)\,\mathrm{d}\xi=\frac{\pi^{2}a}{2\omega\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime}),

(B.23)

to see that

\displaystyle\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}4\pi^{2}k_{\perp}^{4}\left\{\left[\mathrm{i}(2\omega)|V_{\omega k_{\perp}}|^{2}\right]\frac{\pi^{2}a}{2\omega\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\right\}\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})

\displaystyle=\left(|V_{\omega k_{\perp}}|^{2}\frac{4\pi^{4}ak_{\perp}^{4}}{\kappa^{2}\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}\right)\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}).

(B.24)

Therefore, if we choose

V_{\omega k_{\perp}}=\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{2}\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}ak_{\perp}^{4}}}=\frac{\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{4\pi^{4}a}},

(B.25)

the modes are normalized, i.e.,
\langle h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{v},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})
.

The normalization of the scalar sector proceeds in similar fashion, we can write the current as

\displaystyle W_{\lambda}[h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}]

\displaystyle=\overline{h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha\beta}}\nabla_{\lambda}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(\overline{h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}}\nabla_{\lambda}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\overline{h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{ij}}\nabla_{\lambda}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{ij}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{ij}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}}.

(B.26)

Each line on the right-hand side of this can be analyzed separately. We can
start by the easy terms

\overline{h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{ij}}\nabla_{\lambda}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{ij}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{ij}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ij}}\\
=-k_{\perp}^{2}k_{\perp}^{\prime 2}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}}\right)\overline{\mathbb{S}^{ij}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}}\mathbb{S}_{ij}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}},

(B.27)

\overline{h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}}\nabla_{\lambda}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{\alpha j}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha j}}\\
=\frac{k_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}{4}\!\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{(\mathrm{sv})}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)\overline{\mathbb{S}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{j}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}}_{j},

(B.28)

and the more complicated one

\overline{h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}^{\alpha\beta}}\nabla_{\lambda}h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}-h_{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{\alpha\beta}}\\
=\left[\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}-\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right.\\
-\left.\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}k_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}{2}\left(\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}-\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)\right]\overline{\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime}}.

(B.29)

We can see that
\langle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}
will depend on the integrals

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\overline{\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}}\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}=4\pi^{2}\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}),

(B.30)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\overline{\mathbb{S}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{i}}\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{i}=\frac{(k_{x}k_{x}^{\prime}+k_{y}k_{y}^{\prime})}{k_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{\prime}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\!\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}=4\pi^{2}\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}),

(B.31)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\overline{\mathbb{S}_{\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}^{ij}}\,\mathbb{S}^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}}_{ij}&=\frac{(k_{x}^{2}-k_{y}^{2})(k_{x}^{\prime 2}-k_{y}^{\prime 2})+(2k_{x}k_{y})(2k^{\prime}_{x}k^{\prime}_{y})}{2k_{\perp}^{2}k_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\!\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{\perp}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}\\
&=2\pi^{2}\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp}).\end{aligned}

and thus, we have

\langle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}[\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})]\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi\left(A^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}+2B^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}+\frac{C^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}}{2}\right),

(B.32)

where

\displaystyle A^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{\perp}^{4}}{2}\left(\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right),

(B.33a)

\displaystyle B^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{4}\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{(\mathrm{sv})}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla^{\alpha}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right),

(B.33b)

\displaystyle C^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-k_{\perp}^{4}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right).

(B.33c)

We can immediately reduce
B^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}
using
the result of Eq. (
B.20
),
obtaining

B^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}=\frac{k_{\perp}^{4}}{4}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right)\\
+k_{\perp}^{2}\partial_{\xi}\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right).

(B.34)

The other term that needs some work is
A^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}
. First, using integration by parts and commuting some derivatives

\displaystyle A^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\alpha}\left(\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-k_{\perp}^{2}\left(\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}+\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{\perp}^{4}}{2}\left(\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right),

(B.35)

where we will use Eq. (
B.20
)
for the middle term of the right-hand side and will develop more the first one,
to avoid carrying out all these for too long. Opening up the covariant divergence:

\nabla_{\alpha}\left(\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)\\
=\nabla_{\lambda}\left(\nabla^{\lambda}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\lambda}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right)\\
+\nabla_{\xi}\left(\nabla^{\xi}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\xi}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right).

(B.36)

We can see the first term on the right-hand side is identically null (just by lowering the
\lambda
index inside the parenthesis). On the other hand, we can
use the definition of the covariant derivative explicitly, which yields

\nabla_{\alpha}\big(\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\big)\\
=\partial_{\xi}\left(\nabla^{\xi}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\xi}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\right),

(B.37)

a border term, and thus, it does not contribute to the integral result, and thus, after some algebra we can see that

A^{\omega\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}_{\lambda}=\partial_{\xi}\Bigg[\nabla^{\xi}\nabla^{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}-\nabla^{\xi}\nabla^{\beta}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\beta}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\\
-k_{\perp}^{2}\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\nabla^{\xi}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega k_{\perp}}}-\nabla_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\nabla^{\xi}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right)\Bigg]\\
-\frac{k_{\perp}^{4}}{2}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right).

(B.38)

We then find that the inner product reduces to

\langle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{\perp})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi^{2}k_{\perp}^{4}}{\kappa^{2}}\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\right).

(B.39)

Using Eq. (
3.92
) we find

\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\partial_{\lambda}\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}-\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega{k}_{\perp}}}\partial_{\lambda}\Omega_{\mathrm{s}}^{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}\\
=\overline{S_{\omega k_{\perp}}}S_{\omega^{\prime}k_{\perp}}[\mathrm{i}(\omega+\omega^{\prime})]\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\lambda}\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a)\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}\omega^{\prime}/a}(k_{\perp}\mathrm{e}^{a\xi}/a).

(B.40)

and after applying
Eq. (
B.23
) again, we find
that by choosing

S_{\omega k_{\perp}}=\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{2}\sinh(\pi\omega/a)}{a\pi^{4}k_{\perp}^{4}}},

(B.41)

the scalar sector will satisfy
\langle h^{(\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})},h^{(\mathrm{s}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})}\rangle_{\text{t}}=\delta(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}-\mathbf{k}_{\perp}^{\prime})
.

B.3
Derivation of the identity to find the coefficients of the classical expansion

Let us compute the functional

I[h,T]\coloneqq-\kappa^{2}\iiiint_{M}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,T_{ab}\overline{{h}^{ab}}.

(B.42)

As the stress-energy tensor is compactly supported, we can eliminate the causal
past of
\Sigma_{-}
of the integration domain as it will not contribute to the
functional, this is

I[h,T]=-\kappa^{2}\iiiint_{J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,T^{ab}\,\overline{{h}_{ab}}.

(B.43)

On the other hand, in the causal future of
\Sigma_{-}
, the advanced solution
(written in the TT gauge) will satisfy

\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}AT^{ab}-2\mathchoice{R^{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}AT^{cd}=-\kappa^{2}\,T^{ab}.

(B.44)

In this region Eq. (
B.44
) is
satisfied, therefore

I[h,T]=\iiiint_{J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,(\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}\!AT^{ab}-2\mathchoice{R^{{{c}{a}{b}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{c}{a}{b}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{c}{a}{b}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{c}{a}{b}{d}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}AT_{cd})\,\overline{{h}_{ab}}.

(B.45)

Let us work the integrand a little using Leibniz rule to allow us to take
advantage of Eq. (
3.8
)

\displaystyle\overline{{h}_{ab}}\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}AT^{ab}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{c}(\overline{{h}_{ab}}\nabla^{c}AT^{ab})-(\nabla_{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}\nabla^{c}AT^{ab})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{c}(\overline{h_{ab}}\nabla^{c}AT^{ab})-\left[\nabla_{c}(\nabla^{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}\,AT^{ab})-\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}AT^{ab}\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{c}\left(\overline{{h}_{ab}}\,\nabla^{c}AT^{ab}-\nabla^{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}\,AT^{ab}\right)+\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}AT^{ab}.

(B.46)

We recognize the form of the generalized Klein-Gordon current defined in
Eq. (
3.94
), i.e.,

\overline{{h}_{ab}}\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}AT^{ab}=\nabla_{c}W^{c}[h,AT]+\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}AT^{ab},

(B.47)

And thus, using Gauss theorem and Eq. (
3.13
) we find

I[h,T]=\iiiint_{J^{+}(\Sigma_{-})}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\left[\partial_{c}\!\left(\sqrt{-g}W^{c}[h,AT]\right)+AT^{ab}\cancelto{0}{\left(\nabla_{c}\nabla^{c}\overline{{h}_{ab}}-2\mathchoice{R^{{{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\bar{h}_{cd}\right)}\right]\\
=\cancelto{0}{\iiint_{\mathscr{I}^{+}}\mathrm{d}^{3}\Sigma\,n_{c}^{(\mathscr{I}^{+})}W^{c}[h,AT]}+\cancelto{0}{\iiint_{i^{0}}\mathrm{d}^{3}\Sigma\,n_{c}^{(i^{0})}W^{c}[h,AT]}+\iiint_{\Sigma_{-}}\mathrm{d}^{3}\Sigma\,n_{c}W^{c}[h,AT]\\
=0+\left(\frac{2\kappa^{2}}{\mathrm{i}}\right)\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\kappa^{2}}\iiint_{\Sigma_{-}}\mathrm{d}^{3}\Sigma\,n_{c}W^{c}[h,AT]\right)=-2\mathrm{i}\kappa^{2}\langle h,AT\rangle_{\text{t}}.

(B.48)

Using Eq. (
3.125
) we see that this proves Eq. (
3.126
),
an appropriate generalization of Wald’s identity to the gravitational case.

B.4
Convergence of the contributions by inertial parts of the motion

We can write the inertial contributions as the sum of two integrals using
inertial coordinates

\mathcal{I}^{\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}=\overbrace{\int_{-\infty}^{-a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}\mathrm{d}t\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{x}\,T_{L}^{ab}(t,\mathbf{x})\,\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x})}}^{\mathcal{I}^{\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{-}}\\
+\underbrace{\int^{\infty}_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}\mathrm{d}t\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}^{3}\mathbf{x}\,T_{L}^{ab}(t,\mathbf{x})\,\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}(t,\mathbf{x})}}_{\mathcal{I}^{\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{+}},

(B.49)

In these regions, the 4-velocity is

u^{a}_{\pm}=\bm{(}\cosh(aT),0,0,\pm\sinh(aT)\bm{)}

(B.50)

where the
+
sign corresponds to the future after acceleration and the
-

to the past before acceleration. In both of these we have the forcing is
F^{a}=0
, and the trajectory is

\displaystyle\chi^{t}_{\pm}(\tau)=\pm a^{-1}\sinh(aT)+(\tau\mp T)\cosh(aT),

(B.51)

\displaystyle\chi^{z}_{\pm}(\tau)=a^{-1}\cosh(aT)\pm(\tau\mp T)\sinh(aT).

(B.52)

We have to be careful now with the components of the stress-energy tensor as the
integrals are going to be different. For after the acceleration we have

\displaystyle T_{L,+}^{tt}(t,\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=m\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\theta(L-|t|)\int_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau(u^{t})^{2}\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}}

\displaystyle=m\cosh^{2}(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}},

(B.53)

T_{L,+}^{tz}(t,\mathbf{x})=T_{L,+}^{zt}(t,\mathbf{x})\\
=m\cosh(aT)\sinh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}},

(B.54)

T_{L,+}^{zz}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\sinh^{2}(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}},

(B.55)

while for the past

T_{L,-}^{tt}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\cosh^{2}(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int^{-T}_{-\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{-}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{-}(\tau){\bm{)}},

(B.56)

T_{L,-}^{tz}(t,\mathbf{x})=T_{L,-}^{zt}(t,\mathbf{x})\\
=-m\cosh(aT)\sinh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int^{-T}_{-\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{-}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{-}(\tau){\bm{)}},

(B.57)

T_{L,-}^{zz}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\sinh^{2}(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int^{-T}_{-\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\delta{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{-}(\tau){\bm{)}}\delta{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{-}(\tau){\bm{)}}.

(B.58)

From this we see that the results depend on the values of
\tau
that zeroes
the functions appearing inside the Dirac deltas. Let us define

f_{\pm}(t,\tau)\coloneqq t-\chi_{\pm}^{t}(\tau)=t\mp a^{-1}\sinh(aT)-(\tau\mp T)\cosh(aT).

(B.59)

We can find the derivative with respect to
\tau

\frac{\partial f_{\pm}(t,\tau)}{\partial\tau}=-\cosh(aT),

(B.60)

and if we define
t_{\pm}
by
f_{\pm}(t,\tau_{\pm})=0
, we find

\tau_{\pm}(t)=\pm T+\frac{t\mp a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}{\cosh(aT)}.

(B.61)

We can also find the position at this instant

\displaystyle\chi^{z}_{\pm}\bm{(}\tau_{\pm}(t)\bm{)}

\displaystyle=a^{-1}\cosh(aT)\pm\bm{(}\tau_{\pm}(t)\mp T\bm{)}\sinh(aT)

\displaystyle=a^{-1}\cosh(aT)\pm\left[\left(\pm T+\frac{t\mp a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}{\cosh(aT)}\right)\mp T\right]\sinh(aT)

\displaystyle=a^{-1}\cosh(aT)-a^{-1}\sinh(aT)\tanh(aT)\pm t\tanh(aT)

\displaystyle=a^{-1}\sech(aT)\pm t\tanh(aT).

(B.62)

With this in hand we can make the replacements

\delta\,{\bm{(}}t-\chi^{t}_{\pm}(\tau){\bm{)}}=\frac{\delta(\tau-\tau_{\pm})}{\cosh(aT)},

(B.63)

which are satisfied within the limits of each integral for the allowed values of

t
, and thus, for after the acceleration we have

\displaystyle T_{L,+}^{tt}(t,\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=m\cosh^{2}(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\int_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\frac{\delta(\tau-\tau_{+})}{\cosh(aT)}\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{+}(\tau){\bm{)}}

\displaystyle=m\cosh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\,\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-\chi^{z}_{+}(\tau_{+}){\bm{)}}

\displaystyle=m\cosh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\,\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)-t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}},

(B.64)

\displaystyle T_{L,+}^{tz}(t,\mathbf{x})=T_{L,+}^{zt}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\sinh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\,\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)-t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}},

(B.65)

\displaystyle T_{L,+}^{zz}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\sinh^{2}(aT)\sech(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\,\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)-t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}},

(B.66)

while for the past

\displaystyle T_{L,-}^{tt}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\cosh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)+t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}},

(B.67)

\displaystyle T_{L,-}^{tz}(t,\mathbf{x})=T_{L,-}^{zt}(t,\mathbf{x})=-m\sinh(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)+t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}},

(B.68)

\displaystyle T_{L,-}^{zz}(t,\mathbf{x})=m\sinh^{2}(aT)\sech(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)+t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}}.

(B.69)

We can also find the components we need of the Unruh modes using Eq. (
3.123
),

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{tt}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\nabla_{t}\nabla_{t}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g_{tt}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right),

(B.70)

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{tz}=W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{tz}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\nabla_{t}\nabla_{z}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g_{tz}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\partial_{t}\partial_{z}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}},

(B.71)

\displaystyle W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{zz}=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\nabla_{z}\nabla_{z}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g_{zz}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right)=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\partial_{z}\partial_{z}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}w^{\sigma}_{\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}\right).

(B.72)

We can now use the integral form of the scalar Unruh
modes (
1.47
) to write these explicitly and conjugate them

\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{tt}}\\
=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}}{4\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\left(\cosh^{2}\vartheta+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right)\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)],

(B.73)

\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{tz}}=\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{zt}}\\
=-\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}}{4\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\!\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\left(\cosh\vartheta\sinh\vartheta\right)\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)],

(B.74)

\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{zz}}\\
=\frac{2\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}}{4\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\left(\sinh^{2}\vartheta-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right)\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)],

(B.75)

and we can construct

\displaystyle T_{L,+}^{ab}\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m\kappa}{k_{\perp}^{2}2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}\sech(aT)\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})

\displaystyle\quad\times\delta{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)-t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\Bigg[\cosh^{2}(aT)\left(\cosh^{2}\vartheta+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right)-2\cosh(aT)\sinh(aT)\cosh\vartheta\sinh\vartheta

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\qquad+\sinh^{2}(aT)\left(\sinh^{2}\vartheta-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{m\kappa\sech(aT)}{k_{\perp}^{2}2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\delta\,{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)-t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\cosh^{2}(\vartheta-aT)+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right),

(B.76)

and we can compute the integral of this that reduces to. Analogously, we have for the past

T_{L,-}^{ab}\overline{W^{(\sigma,\mathrm{s},\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp})}_{ab}}\\
=\frac{m\kappa\sech(aT)}{k_{\perp}^{2}2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{\perp}}\,\theta(L-|t|)\,\delta^{2}(\mathbf{x}_{\perp})\delta{\bm{(}}z-a^{-1}\sech(aT)+t\tanh(aT){\bm{)}}\\
\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}(z\sinh\vartheta-t\cosh\vartheta)]\left(\cosh^{2}(\vartheta+aT)+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right),

(B.77)

from where we find

\mathcal{I}^{\sigma,\omega\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}_{-}=\frac{m\kappa\sech(aT)}{k_{\perp}^{2}2\pi^{2}\sqrt{2a}}\\
\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(-1)^{\sigma}\vartheta\omega/a}\left(\cosh^{2}(\vartheta+aT)+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\right)\exp[-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}a^{-1}\sech(aT)\sinh\vartheta]\\
\times\int_{-L}^{-a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}\mathrm{d}t\exp\{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta+\cosh\vartheta]\}.

(B.78)

Let us define the integrals

\displaystyle f_{+}(\vartheta,T.L)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int^{L}_{a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}\mathrm{d}t\exp\{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta-\cosh\vartheta]\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{k_{\perp}[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta-\cosh\vartheta]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Big(\exp\{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}a\sinh(aT)[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta-\cosh\vartheta]\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\exp\{-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}L[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta-\cosh\vartheta]\}\Big),

(B.79)

and

f_{-}(\vartheta,T,L)\coloneqq\int_{-L}^{-a^{-1}\sinh(aT)}\mathrm{d}t\exp\{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}t[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta+\cosh\vartheta]\}\\
=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{k_{\perp}[\tanh(aT)\sinh\vartheta+\cosh\vartheta]}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\\
